ORANGE AND ROCKLAND UTILITIES, INC. 
EMBEDDED COST OF SERVICE STUDY 
ELECTRIC DEPARTMENT 
YEAR 2019 
 
 
I - SUMMARY 
The Embedded Cost of Service (ECOS) study allocates Orange and Rockland’s Utilities, Inc., (O&R) 
Electric Department’s costs among their respective service classifications based on an analysis of the rate 
base, including book cost of plant, and the operating expenses, including operation and maintenance for the 
Electric Department for the calendar year 2019. The ECOS study methodology is based on a two-step 
procedure. First, the costs are functionalized and classified to Operating Functions, as shown in the ECOS 
study on Tables 2 through 5. Along with traditional functions, the study also includes fully unbundled 
competitive merchant function, meter ownership, printing and mailing a bill and receipts processing 
functions. The costs within each function are then allocated to Customer Classes based on appropriate 
physical quantities, such as kW demand, or other appropriate bases of allocation, such as book cost of 
meters. The bases of cost allocation are shown in detail on Table 7 as Allocation Factors and are further 
described below. The details of allocations by customer class are shown on Tables 2 through 5. The results 
of the cost allocation step are combined with class revenues to yield the rate of return statement by class 
shown on Table 1. The monthly average Customer Costs by Class are shown on Table 6.  The costs 
allocated in the ECOS study reflect 2019 booked data.  The revenues reflect current rates, i.e. rates 
effective January 1, 2021. This adjustment is needed to ensure that the study reflects current rate levels and 
shows the correct relationship among service classifications. These revenues comprise the annual sales 
revenues for Orange and Rockland’s service. They exclude all MSC/ECA, System Benefits Charge (SBC) 
and Renewable Portfolio Standards (RPS) revenues, and represent merchant function, competitive 
metering, billing and payment processing, transmission, distribution, and customer revenue requirement.  
For purposes of unbundling, total revenues are used to allocate customer-care related expenses, i.e., credit 
& collections, to competitive functions as determined by the Public Service Commission’s Unbundling 
Order. 
Exhibit___DAC-2
Page 4 of 333


--- Page 5 ---
II - DESCRIPTION OF OPERATING FUNCTIONS – Tables 2, 3 and 5  
The operating functions shown on Table 2, Rate Base, Table 3, Operating Expenses, and Table 5, 
Income Taxes (State and Federal) are described below. Where applicable, these functions include fixed 
costs including an allocation of common plant, associated operating expenses, A & G expenses, state 
income taxes (“SIT”) and federal income taxes (“FIT”). 
Line 1, Merchant Function 
The Merchant function includes the costs associated with commodity billing and analysis, and commodity 
procurement and scheduling. Also included is a revenue-based allocation of credit & collections, IT, and 
education & promotional advertising.  This function is allocated to classes based on a hybrid allocator, 
which includes procurement costs being allocated 100% on annual full-service sales, while the revenue-
based adders of credit & collections, IT and education & promotional advertising are allocated 25% on 
annual full-service sales and 75% on number of full-service customers.   
Line 2, Transmission 
The Transmission function represents the fixed costs for interconnecting the transmission system to 
generating stations and other utilities.  
Line 3, Substation 
The Substation function represents the fixed costs associated with Land and Land Rights, Structures and 
Improvements and Station Equipment. 
High Tension System 
Line 4, High Tension OH/UG - Demand  
Line 5, High Tension OH/UG - Customer 
The High-Tension function includes the costs of primary feeders that provide the source of supply from 
substations to high tension customers and radial transformers.  These costs are further divided into a 
demand and customer component using a minimum system concept. 
Exhibit___DAC-2
Page 5 of 333


--- Page 6 ---
Low Tension Distribution System: 
 Line 6, Transformers – O.H. Demand 
 Line 7, Transformers – U.G. Demand 
The fixed costs for the above functions are subdivided to show separately the functions associated with the 
overhead (OH) and underground (UG) line transformers. The demand component includes the portion of 
transformers above the minimum system. 
 Line 8, Transformers – O.H. Customer 
 Line 9, Transformers – U.G. Customer 
The fixed costs for these functions are considered to be joint customer costs as distinguished from direct 
customer costs, since they represent the estimated costs of the minimum-size, jointly-used line transformers 
needed to connect the customers to the distribution system under the existing conditions of customer 
density and geographical dispersion. Expressed in another manner, the customer component is the cost of 
the smallest secondary system theoretically needed to physically connect all the existing service points.  
Line 10, O.H. Lines Demand  
Line 11, U.G. Lines Demand 
The fixed costs for the above functions are subdivided to show separately the functions associated with the 
overhead (OH) and underground (UG) lines. The demand component includes the secondary system for 
OH and UG Lines above the minimum system. 
 Line 12, O.H. Lines Customer 
 Line 13, U.G. Lines Customer 
The fixed costs for these functions are considered to be joint customer costs as distinguished from direct 
customer costs, since they represent the estimated costs of the minimum-size, jointly-used distribution lines 
needed to connect the customers to the distribution system under the existing conditions of customer 
density and geographical dispersion. Expressed in another manner, the customer component is the cost of 
the smallest secondary system theoretically needed to physically connect all the existing service points. 
Exhibit___DAC-2
Page 6 of 333


--- Page 7 ---
Line 14, Services – O.H.  
Line 15, Services – U.G.  
These fixed costs represent overhead and underground service connections. The costs for these functions 
are considered direct customer costs since service connections attach the customer to the distribution 
system.  
Line 16, Meter Ownership  
The Meter Ownership function includes the fixed costs for metering equipment on customers’ premises, 
plus meters and meter devices carried in stock.  Costs for this function are considered direct customer costs. 
Also included is a revenue-based allocation of credit & collection, uncollectibles and education & 
promotional advertising.  
Line 17, Utility Metering 
Utility metering consists of costs associated with the fixed cost for meter installations that are not 
competitive and will remain with the utility.  Costs for this function are considered direct customer costs.  
Line 18, Install. on Custr. Premises 
The Installations on Customers’ Premises function includes the fixed costs for equipment installed on  
customers’ premises. These costs are considered direct customer costs. 
Line 19, Street Lighting  
The Street Lighting function includes the fixed costs for street lighting and signal systems. 
Line 20, Customer Accounting 
The Customer Accounting function consists of direct customer costs of customer records and collection 
expenses and meter reading.  The function was reduced for Receipts Processing and Printing & Mailing a 
Bill, as these costs were assigned directly to their respective functions. Credit and collections costs were 
allocated to competitive functions based on a revenue allocation. The remaining costs, which are direct 
customer costs were assigned to customer classes based on the number of meters and total number of 
customers as applicable.   
Line 21, Printing & Mailing a Bill  
The Printing & Mailing a Bill function consists of the customer accounting expense of billing 
Exhibit___DAC-2
Page 7 of 333


--- Page 8 ---
customers, as well as allocations for call center and service center operations and IT, all based on a detailed 
study of those activities. Also included is a revenue-based allocation of credit & collection, uncollectibles 
and education & promotional advertising. Printing & Mailing a Bill is a direct customer cost.  This function 
is allocated to classes based on the number of bills. 
Line 22, Receipts Processing  
The Receipts Processing function consists of the customer accounting expense of accepting customer  
payments.  It also includes allocations of call center and service center operations and IT, all based on 
detailed study of those activities. Also included is a revenue-based allocation of credit & collection, 
uncollectibles, and education & promotional advertising. Receipts Processing is a direct customer cost.  
This function is allocated to classes based on the number of bills. 
Line 23, Uncollectibles 
The Uncollectibles function includes the operation and maintenance expenses for uncollectibles accounts. 
Uncollectibles associated with commodity have been adjusted out of the study.  A portion of uncollectibles 
was allocated on a revenue basis to competitive functions. The remaining expense, shown on Line 23, has 
been allocated to classes on a revenue basis to reflect the comparative relationship between the uncollectible 
characteristics of residential classes (0.73%) and non-residential classes (0.22%).  
Line 24, Customer Service 
The Customer Service function consists of customer assistance expenses, informational and instructional  
expenses, miscellaneous customer service and information expense.  Educational customer advertising 
costs were allocated to competitive functions based on a revenue allocation. The remaining costs, which are 
direct customer costs were allocated to the customer classes based on the number of customers. 
Line 25, Revenues  
The Revenue function is non-applicable for this study.

III - DESCRIPTION OF ALLOCATION FACTORS - Table 7 
Factor  
  
Description and Source 
D01  
 
Transmission 
The D01, Transmission, allocation factor, summer system peak 
responsibility demand, is based on the highest five day, four –hour 
averages.  
D04 
Substation  
The D04, Substation, allocation factor is based on the Non-coincident 
maximum high tension class demand at the system level. 
D02 
  
  
High Tension - Demand 
The D02, High Tension, allocation factor is based on the Non-
coincident maximum high tension class demand at the system level.  
D03 
 
 
 
Low Tension - Overhead & Underground 
 
The D03, Low Tension – Overhead and Underground, allocation 
factor is based on the average of non-coincident maximum class 
demands and individual customer billing demands for summer and 
winter seasons.  
E01 
kWh Sales 
 
 
 
 
 
The E01, Annual kWh Sales, is the total annual kilowatt-hour sales  
                                                            for O&R service classes. 
 C01                                           
Overhead and Underground Lines Customer - Primary  
The C01 Primary – Customer Component allocation factor represents 
the sum of customer-related primary overhead assets, allocated to 
service classes on the basis of the number of overhead services, and 
customer-related primary underground assets, allocated to classes on 
the basis of the number of underground services.     
Exhibit___DAC-2
Page 9 of 333


--- Page 10 ---
C02 
Overhead Lines Customer - Secondary  
C03 
Underground Lines Customer - Secondary 
C02 and C03 allocation factors are based on the number of overhead 
and underground services excluding primary, commercial and 
industrial classes.  The number of actual services installed for each 
class was estimated based on a statistically significant sample of 
service connections across O&R’s service territory.  By cross-
referencing records of customer locations in the Company’s mapping, 
property records and customer billing systems, we were able to 
determine the number of services used in connecting customers in the 
sample to the Company’s distribution system.  
C04 
Book Cost – Services Overhead  
C05 
Book Cost – Services Underground 
The C04 and C05 allocation factors are based on the year end book 
cost for overhead and underground services developed using the 
sample services study. The cost of services installed for each class 
was estimated based on a statistically significant sample of service 
connections across O&R’s service territory.  By cross-referencing 
records of customer locations in the Company’s mapping, property 
records and customer billing systems, we were able to determine the 
book cost of services used in connecting customers in the sample to 
the Company’s distribution system.  
C06 
Book Cost - Install. on Customers’ Premises  
The C06, Installations on Customers’ Premises, allocation factor is 
based on the number of customers excluding lighting, commercial and 
industrial service classifications, as well as West Point.  
Exhibit___DAC-2
Page 10 of 333


--- Page 11 ---
C07 
Book Cost – Street Lighting 
The C07, Street Lighting, allocation factor is allocated to the 
applicable lighting classes based on their respective individual 
customer maximum demands. 
S02 
Number of Bills Customer 
The S02, allocation factor was developed based on number of 
customer bills and is used to allocate to the service classes the 
Printing and Mailing a Bill and Receipts Processing functions. 
S03 
Customer Accounting 
The S03, Customer Accounting, allocation factor was developed by 
allocating the PSC accounts that comprise the total customer 
accounting expenses, excluding receipts processing and printing and 
mailing a bill.  The allocation factor consists of PSC Account 902, 
Meter Reading, allocated to the service classes based on the number 
of meters, PSC Account 903, Customer Records, allocated based on 
the number of customers, PSC Account 901, Supervision, and PSC 
Account 905, Miscellaneous, allocated based on the sum of 
allocations of PSC Account 902 and PSC Account 903. 
S04 
Customer Service Expenses 
The S04, Customer Service Expenses, allocation factor is allocated 
based on number of customers. 
S05 
Meter Ownership 
The S05, Meter Ownership allocation factor is the year-end book cost 
of meters based on a detailed study of customers’ meters for each 
service classification is used to allocate the costs associated with the 
Meter Ownership function.

S06 
 
 
 
 
Utility Metering 
The S06 allocator is used to allocate meter installations costs that are 
not deemed competitive and will remain with the utility. 
 
 
S012                                               Uncollectibles Accounts  
                                                           The S012, Uncollectibles, allocation factor is developed based on the  
                                                           uncollectible characteristics of residential and non-residential classes. 
                                                           The Uncollectibles function includes the operation and  
                                                            maintenance expenses for uncollectibles accounts, excluding          
                                                            uncollectibles associated with commodity.  
R01                                          
Revenues from Sales 
The R01, Revenues from Sales are annual T&D revenues (2019 
annual sales priced out at current T&D rates) used in the study.   
R02                                          
  
Other Electric Revenues 
                                                     The R02, Other Electric Revenues are comprised of miscellaneous 
revenue items allocated to the revenue function.  
R03 
 
  
Competitive MFC Revenues 
 
 
 
The R03, Competitive MFC Revenues are the revenues associated 
with the Merchant Function Charge (excluding uncollectibles 
associated with commodity). 
R04 
 
  
Competitive Metering Revenues 
 
 
 
The R04, Competitive Metering Revenues are the revenues associated 
with competitive metering. 
R05 
 
  
Competitive BPP Revenues 
 
 
 
The R05, Competitive BBP Revenues are the revenues associated 
with competitive billing and payment processing. 
 
Exhibit___DAC-2

--- Page 13 ---
U01 
Unbundled Allocator 
U01, the Unbundled Allocator, is used to allocate the unbundled 
Merchant function.  It is composed of procurement costs (allocated to 
service classes based on full service annual kWh sales); commodity-
related credit & collections, IT and education & promotional 
advertising costs and are all allocated to classes based on 25% full-
service sales and 75% full-service customers. 
K01
Number of Customers 
K01, Number of Customers, is the annual number of customers  
for O&R service classes. 
IV - DESCRIPTION OF CUSTOMER CLASSES 
The customer classes consist of:  Total Company, Total Residential, Total C&I, Total C&I Primary, Total 
Municipal Street Lighting, Total Municipal & Private Traffic Lighting, Total Commercial,  
Total Lighting,  Total Industrial,  West Point,  Residential S.C. No. 1 – General,  Residential S.C. No. 19 – 
Voluntary TOU,  C&I S.C. No. 2 – Secondary,  C&I S.C. No. 2 – Primary,  C&I S.C. No. 2 – With Space 
Heating,  C&I S.C. No. 2 – Secondary Non Demand Billed,  C&I S.C. No. 20 – Secondary Voluntary 
TOU,   C&I S.C. No. 3 Primary,  C&I S.C. No. 21 – Primary Voluntary TOU,   S.C. No. 4 – Municipal 
Street Lighting,  S.C. No. 5 – Municipal and Private Traffic Lighting,  S. C 6 – Municipal Owned Street 
Lighting, S.C. No. 9 – Commercial,  S.C. No. 16 – Public & Private Lighting,  S.C. No. 16 – Energy 
Lighting,  S.C. No. 22 – Industrial.

system rate of return percentage. 
Line 21, Deviation 
The Deviation is the extent (in percentage points) by which the actual rate of return for each 
customer class deviates from the total system rate of return. 
Line 23 and 24, ± 10% Tolerance Bands 
A +10 tolerance band has been computed around the system rate of return and are shown on Lines 
23 and 24, respectively.   
Lines 26 and 27, Revenue Surplus and Deficiency     
The Revenue Surplus or Deficiency for the returns that fall outside the tolerance band are shown on Lines 
26 and 27, respectively. 
VI - RATE BASE - Table 2 
Total Rate Base shown on Table 2, Pages 57 through 63, is the sum of book costs related to Plant in 
Service, Table 2, Pages 1 through 7, (transmission and distribution plants), General Plant/Common 
Plant, Table 2, Pages 8 through 14 and Plant Held for Future Use, Table 2 Pages 15 through 21 less the 
corresponding Accumulated Reserve for Depreciation, Table 2, Pages 22 through 28 plus Non-
Interest Bearing CWIP, Table 2, pages 29 through 35 resulting in Net Plant, Table 2, pages 36 to 42 
minus Rate Base Adjustments, Table 2, pages 43 to 49 plus Working Capital, Table 2, Pages 50 
through 56.   
Plant in Service – Table 2, Pages 1 through 7: 
The total book costs for electric plant in service shown on Table 2, Pages 1 through 7 are costs comprised 
of the transmission and distribution plant in service book costs from the Company’s accounting data 
organized by PSC Account shown functionalized on the workpaper Total Book Cost Summary. 
Line 1 through Line 4, PSC Account 301 to PSC Account 303 – Intangible Plant 
These costs were functionalized based on total common plant book cost. 
Exhibit___DAC-2
Page 15 of 333


--- Page 16 ---
Line 8, PSC Account 310 through PSC Account 346  - Production  
The book costs for production plant is zero. 
Lines 11 through 20, PSC Account 350 through PSC Account 359 – Transmission 
These costs were functionalized direct to the transmission function.  
Line 26, PSC Account 360, Land and Land Rights  
The cost is for land occupied by substations and distribution facilities and was functionalized to the 
Substation function.  
Line 27, PSC Account 361, Structures and Improvements 
Line 28, PSC Account 362, Station Equipment 
The costs represent the substation structures and equipment plant. These costs were functionalized to the 
Substation function.  
Line 29, PSC Account 364, Poles, Towers and Fixtures  
The high-tension portion of these costs was divided into a high-tension demand and customer component 
using a minimum system methodology. The low-tension plant was further subdivided into demand and 
customer components using a minimum system methodology.  The results of the minimum size method for 
PSC Account 365, (for both primary and secondary) Overhead Conductors were applied to this account. 
Line 30, PSC Account 365, Overhead Conductors 
Quantities and costs of overhead cable assets consisting of high tension (primary) and low tension 
(secondary) cables were provided by Property Records tabulated as PSC Account 365, Overhead 
Conductors. The total book cost shown on Line 30, PSC Account 365, Overhead Conductors was then 
further subdivided into their respective demand and customer components using a minimum system 
methodology.  
Line 31, PSC Account 366, Underground Conduit  
Line 32, PSC Account 367, Underground Conductors  
In an identical manner as explained above for overhead cable, quantities and costs of underground cable  
consisting of high tension (primary) and low tension (secondary) cable were provided by Property Records 
Exhibit___DAC-2
Page 16 of 333


--- Page 17 ---
tabulated as PSC Account 367, Underground Conductors. The total book cost shown on Line 32, PSC 
Account 367, Underground Conductors was then further subdivided into their respective demand and 
customer components using a minimum system methodology.  The total book cost, Line 31, PSC Account 
366, Underground Conduit was then assigned to the high tension and low tension demand and customer 
component functions based on the allocations developed above for PSC Account 367, Underground 
Conductors.    
Line 33, PSC Account 368, Line Transformers  
This represents the functionalized total book cost of the overhead and underground line transformers.  
These costs were further subdivided into demand and customer components using a minimum system 
methodology. The methodology employs the “Minimum Size” method to classify certain distribution plant 
accounts as either customer-related or demand-related.  The minimum size for the Overhead and 
Underground Transformers is determined by calculating the weighted average unit cost using the embedded 
cost of installed transformers of up to and including 150kVa and 250 kVa respectively.    
Line 34, PSC Account 369, Services  
The total book cost of services was directly assigned to the overhead and underground services functions 
based on the Company’s property records data. 
Line 35, PSC Account 370, Meters and Meter Installations  
The total cost of meters and meter installations is functionalized to the Meter Ownership and Utility 
Metering functions.  
 Line 36, PSC Account 371, Installations on Customers’ Premises  
The total book cost for the installations on customer premises was assigned directly to the Installation on 
Customers’ Premises function.  
Line 37, PSC Account 373, Street Lighting and Signal Systems  
The total book cost for street lighting and signal systems was assigned directly to the Street Lighting 
function.

Lines 43 through 53, General Plant - PSC Accounts 389 through 399 
These costs were functionalized to the functions based on Common Plant.  
Line 58, Total Plant 
Total Plant is the sum of Intangible Plant, Production Plant (zero for this study), Transmission Plant, 
Distribution Plant and General Plant. 
Lines 63 through 67, Plant Held for Future Use  
Line 63 through Line 67 represents the functionalization of book cost of plant held for future use based on 
their respective plant in service accounts. 
Lines 74 through 84, Common Plant – PSC Accounts 303.2-303.9, 389 through 398, 
These costs were functionalized to the functions based on labor expenses.  
Line 88, Total Book Cost  
Total Book Cost is the sum of Total Plant, Common Plant and Plant Held for Future Use. 
Accumulated Reserve for Depreciation -Table 2, Pages 22 through 28 
The Accumulated Reserve for Depreciation shown by function on Table 2, Pages 22 through 28 were 
functionalized based on the corresponding book cost of plant.

The year ended balance of Non-Interest Bearing Construction Work in Progress on which interest is not 
capitalized, is shown in total on Line 32 of Table 2, Pages 29 through 35. This amount was functionalized 
based on the book cost of plant shown on Table 2, Pages 1 through 7.

Rate Base Adjustments – Table 2, Pages 43 through 49 
The year ended balance of Rate Base Adjustments is shown on Line 32 of Table 2, Pages 43 through 49.
Working Capital – Table 2, Pages 50 through 56 
Working Capital which appears on Table 2, Pages 50 through 56 is composed of the cost of materials and 
supplies on hand, prepayments of operating taxes, insurance, as well as a cash allowance for operation and 
maintenance expenses representing a lag of revenue collection over payments for costs incurred.  
Total Rate Base – Table 2, Pages 5741 through 63 
Total Rate Base is the sum of Net Plant, Pages 36 through 42, Rate Base Adjustments, Pages 43 through 
49 and Working Capital, Pages 36 through 56, shown on Table 2, Pages through 63. 
VII – OPERATING EXPENSES – Table 3, Pages 1 through 35 
Total Operating Expenses, Table 3, Pages 29 through 35, represent the sum of expenses by function, by 
class, of Operation & Maintenance Expenses Table 3, Pages 1 through 7, and Total Other Expenses 
which is the sum of Depreciation & Amortization Table 3, Pages 8 through 14, Property Taxes Table 3, 
Pages 15 through 21, and Payroll & Misc. Taxes Table 3, Pages 22 through 28.  The major types of 
operation and maintenance expenses are, Transmission, Distribution, Customer Accounting, Uncollectibles, 
and Customer Service including Miscellaneous Revenue Credits and Administrative and General Expenses 
developed and summarized by Line number in the Total Operating Expenses Summary work paper 
below: 
Total Operating Expenses Summary: 
Lines 1, PSC Accounts 500 through 557, Production Expenses  
Production expenses are not reflected in this study. 
Exhibit___DAC-2
Page 19 of 333


--- Page 20 ---
Line 3, PSC Accounts 560 through 572, Transmission Expenses  
These costs represent transmission expenses, functionalized to the Transmission function based on the book 
cost function allocation of transmission plant. 
Line 5, PSC Account 575.7 Regional Marketing Expenses  
This cost is part of transmission maintenance for monitoring and compliance duties. 
Line 8, PSC Account 580, Operation Supervision and Engineering  
The Supervision and Engineering expense related to Operation was reallocated (based on the column titled 
“Reallocation”) to PSC Accounts 581 through 588. 
Line 9, PSC Account 581, Load Dispatching 
These costs are assigned directly to the Transmission function but were zero in this study. 
Line 10, PSC Account 582, Station Expenses  
These costs were functionalized to the Substation function based on their related book cost. 
Line 11, PSC Account 583, Overhead Lines  
These costs were functionalized based on the functionalization of the book costs of PSC Account, 364 
Poles, Towers and Fixtures and PSC Account 365, Overhead Conductors. 
Line 12, PSC Account 584, Underground Lines  
These costs were functionalized based on the book costs of PSC Account 366 Underground Conduit, and 
PSC Account 367, Underground Conductors. 
Line 13, PSC Account 585, Street Lighting  
The costs were zero for this account. 
Line 14, PSC Account 586, Meters  
The costs were functionalized directly to Utility Metering function. 
Line 15, PSC Account 587, Customer Installations Expenses  
The costs were functionalized directly to the Installation on Customers' Premises function.  
Exhibit___DAC-2
Page 20 of 333


--- Page 21 ---
Line 16, PSC Account 588, Miscellaneous Distribution Expenses  
The costs were functionalized based on the book costs of the total distribution plant.  A portion of this 
account was functionalized to the Merchant function.  
Line 17, PSC Account 589, Distribution Rents  
The costs were functionalized based on book costs of total distribution plant. 
Line 18, Total Distribution Operation Expense  
Total of PSC Accounts 580 through 589 (Line 8 through Line 17).  
Line 21, Total Distribution Operation Expense less Rent and S&E 
Total Distribution Operation Expense less Rent and S&E is Line 18 minus Line 17, PSC Account 589, 
Distribution Rents, minus Line 8, Operation Supervision &Engineering. 
Line 27, PSC Account 590, Maintenance Supervision and Engineering  
The costs were zero for this account. 
Line 28, PSC Account 591, Structures  
The costs were zero for this account. 
Line 29, PSC Account 592, Station Equipment  
The costs were functionalized to the Substation function based on PSC Account 362, Station Equipment. 
Line 30, PSC Account 593, Overhead Lines  
The costs were functionalized based on PSC Account 364, Poles, Towers and Fixtures and PSC Account 
365, Overhead Conductors. 
Line 31, PSC Account 594, Underground Lines  
The costs were functionalized based on PSC Account 366, Underground Conduit and PSC Account 367, 
Underground Conductors. 
Line 32, PSC Account 595, Line Transformers  
The costs were functionalized based on book cost of PSC Account 368, Line Transformers and Devices. 
Line 33, PSC Account 596, Street Lighting  
The costs were functionalized directly to the Street Lighting function.  
Exhibit___DAC-2
Page 21 of 333


--- Page 22 ---
Line 34, PSC Account 597, Meters  
The costs were zero for this account. 
Line 35, PSC Account 598, Miscellaneous Distribution Plant  
The costs were zero for this account. 
Line 36, Total Distribution Maintenance Expenses  
Total of PSC Accounts 590 through 598 (Line 27 through Line 35). 
Line 39, Total Distribution Maintenance Expenses less S&E 
Total Distribution Maintenance Expense less S&E is Line 36 minus Line 27. 
Line 42, Total Distribution Expenses 
The sum of Total Distribution Operation Expenses, Line 18 and Total Distribution Maintenance Expenses, 
Line 36.  
Line 46, Total Distribution Expenses Excluding Rents and S&E 
Line 46 is the sum Line 21 and Line 39.  
Lines 49, 50, 51 & 53,  Customer Accounting 
The total customer accounting expenses of PSC Accounts 901, 902, 903, and 905, excluding portions 
allocated to the competitive functions, were functionalized directly to the Customer Accounting function. 
Line 52, Uncollectibles 
PSC Account 904, Uncollectible expenses, excluding commodity related uncollectibles and portions 
allocated to the competitive functions, were functionalized directly to the Uncollectibles function. 
Line 59, PSC Accounts 907 through 916, Customer Service &Sales Expense 
Customer Service expenses, excluding costs related to educational customer advertising that were allocated 
to the competitive functions and costs associated with SBC, were functionalized directly to the Customer 
Service function.  
Line 61, Total Operation and Maintenance Expenses 
Total Operation and Maintenance Expenses is the sum of Lines 1, 3, 5, 42, 54 and 59. 
Exhibit___DAC-2
Page 22 of 333


--- Page 23 ---
Line 66, PSC Accounts 920 through 935, Administrative and General Expenses 
Administrative and General Expenses consist of PSC Accounts 920 through 935. Labor was used as the 
basis of functionalization for PSC Accounts 920, 921, 922, 923, 926, 929, 930.2, 931, and 935. PSC 
Accounts 924, 925 and 928 were functionalized based on transmission and distribution operation and 
maintenance expenses excluding rents.  
Line 69, Adjusted Total O&M 
The Adjusted Total O&M, Line 69 equals the sum of Line 61 and Line 66. 
Line 74, Total Adjusted Miscellaneous Revenue Credits  
The functionalized Miscellaneous Revenue Credits on Line 74 represent the adjusted sum of PSC Accounts 
447, 449, 451, 454 and 456.  
Line 76, Total O & M  
Total Adjusted O&M is the sum of Line 69 Adjusted Total O & M and Line 74 Total Adjusted 
Miscellaneous Revenue Credits. 
Line 80, Payroll & Misc. Taxes  
The Payroll & Miscellaneous Taxes were functionalized on a labor basis. 
Line 81, Property Taxes 
The Property Taxes were functionalized based on total book cost of plant, including common plant. 
Line 82, Depreciation & Amortization 
The Depreciation & Amortization Expenses were identified with each plant PSC Account or group of 
accounts and functionalized in proportion to the corresponding depreciation reserve costs. 
Line 83, Total Other Expenses  
The Total Other Expenses is the sum of Line 80, Payroll & Miscellaneous Taxes, Line 81, Property Taxes 
and Line 82, Depreciation & Amortization.  
Line 86, Total Operating Expenses  
The Total Operating Expenses is the sum of Line 76, Total O&M and Line 83, Total Other Expenses.

--- Page 24 ---
VIII - OPERATING REVENUES – Table 4, Pages 1 through 7 
Operating Revenues are tabulated on Table 4, Pages 1 through 7. The Total Operating Revenues are 
shown on Line 7 calculated by the sum of Line 1 through Line 5 as shown below: 
Line 1, Revenue from Sales 
Revenues from Sales are the annual T&D revenues.  
Line 2, Other Electric Revenues  
Other Electric Revenues are comprised of miscellaneous revenue items allocated to the revenue function. 
Line 3, Competitive MFC Revenues   
Revenues associated with the Merchant Function Charge, excluding commodity uncollectibles. 
Line 4, Competitive Metering Revenues 
Annual competitive metering revenues. 
Line 5, BPP Revenues 
Annual competitive billing and payment processing revenues. 
Line 7, Total Operating Revenues  
Total Operating Revenues are the sum of Line 1 through Line 5.  
IX – STATE AND FEDERAL INCOME TAXES – Table 5, Pages 1 through 28 
State Income Taxes are shown on Table 5, Pages 1 through 14. State Income Tax Computation shown 
on Table 5, Pages 8 through 14 was calculated at 6.50% of taxable income plus SIT Adjustments, Table 
5, Pages 1 through 7. Federal Income Taxes are shown on Table 5, Pages 15 through 28. The Federal 
Income Tax Computation shown on Table 5, Pages 22 through 28 was calculated at 21% of taxable 
income (less SIT) plus FIT Adjustments, Table 5, Pages 15 through 21.  In the SIT Adjustments and 
FIT Adjustments workpapers, each individual deduction/addition tax adjustment line item is multiplied by 
6.50% for SIT and 21% for FIT and is then functionalized based on cost causation.  SIT and FIT amounts 
by function are not the final amounts because they do not include the revenue functional amounts since 
they are not determined until subsequent calculations. Results are presented on a functional basis to 
Exhibit___DAC-2
Page 24 of 333


--- Page 25 ---
maintain a consistent report format. The total state income tax by class is shown on Line 32 of Table 5, 
Pages 8 through 14 and the total federal income tax by class is shown on Line 32 of Table 5, Pages 22 
through 28.  
 
X – CUSTOMER COST BY CLASS - Table 6, Pages 1 through 7 
These are electric system costs considered to be customer related and are shown, by class, on Table 6, 
Pages 1 through 7. 
Line 1, Number of Customers 
The number of customers in each class from the allocation factor K01. 
Line 3, Rate Base 
The customer related rate base shown for each class from Table 2, Pages 57 through 63, Line 29. 
Line 5, Total Customer Operating Exps. 
The customer related operating expenses shown from Table 3, Pages 29 through 35, Line 29. 
Line 6, Monthly Customer Operating Expensess Cost/Customer 
The monthly amount for operating expenses per customer shown on Line 6 is calculated starting 
with Line 5 divided by Line 1, then the results are divided by 12. 
Line 8, Return @ 8.82% (Customer) 
The applied rate of return on rate base of 8.82% is the Total System Rate of Return developed in this study, 
shown on Table 1, Page 1, Column 1, Line 17. 
Line 9, S.I.T. & F.I.T. Percent on Return  
The S.I.T. & F.I.T. Percent on Return, Line 9 developed by dividing the sum of the Total Company State 
and Federal Income Taxes as shown on Table 1, Page 1, Column 1, Line 8 and Line 9, respectively, by 
the Total System Utility Operating Income (return) shown on Table 1, Column 1, Line 13. 
Line 10, Income Tax On Return 
The Return of Line 8 multiplied by the S.I.T. & F.I.T. Percent on Return Line 9, results in the Income 
Taxes on Return on a class-by-class basis. 
Exhibit___DAC-2
Page 25 of 333


--- Page 26 ---
Line 11, Total Return & S.I.T. & F.I.T.  
The Total Return & F.I.T. (including S.I.T.) shown on Line 11 is the sum of Line 8, Return and Line 10, 
Income Tax on Return. 
Line 12, Monthly Ret. S.I.T. & FIT Cost/Cust. 
The monthly amount for Return and Income Taxes, (including State) per customer is calculated starting 
with Line 11 divided by Line 1, then the results are divided by 12. 
Line 14, Monthly Customer Costs 
The Monthly Customer Costs are the sum of Line 6 and Line 12.
